From the dialogue so far and your current candidates, ask the one follow-up question that best separates them.\n\n[Current Turn]\n12\n\n[Current Candidates]\nF22, F60.0, F32.8\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I’m here because things at work have gotten… uncomfortable. I think it’s because of that county safety award. Like they’re trying to discredit me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any other changes in how your coworkers interact with you outside of these report reviews and budget questions?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah. Compliance reviews have gotten more tense, and mentoring the younger inspectors isn’t as easy as it used to be. I’ve also… pulled back from a few coworkers I used to trust. It feels like that’s what put a target on me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're being watched or targeted even when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: Not really outside of work, no. It’s mostly tied to the people there… the reports, the budget questions, that whole thing after the award. I don’t walk around thinking strangers are watching me or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you think anyone else has noticed that your coworkers are trying to discredit you?\n\nPatient: Maybe. I mean… I think some people can see it’s not just routine. A couple coworkers get quiet when the reports come up, like they know what’s going on but don’t want to get involved. My supervisor says it’s routine, but I don’t buy that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people at work are acting against you because of something you've done, or for no logical reason at all?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s for no reason. It started after that county safety award. Since then, certain people have been twisting my inspection reports and asking budget questions like I’m doing something wrong. My supervisor calls it normal review, but… it feels like they’re trying to make me look crooked.\n\nDoctor: Have you considered the possibility that your supervisor is correct and the changes at work are just part of a normal review process?\n\nPatient: I’ve considered it, yeah. I mean… that’s what he keeps saying. But it doesn’t feel normal to me. Not after the award. The way they’ve been going over my reports and asking those budget questions—it feels too pointed, like they’re trying to make me look crooked. I can’t just ignore that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people at work would be doing this to you if you hadn't received the county safety award?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. It really started after that award. Before that, they weren’t picking through my reports like this or asking those budget questions in that way. It feels like that’s what put a target on me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're being watched or targeted even when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: Not really outside of work, no. It’s mostly tied to the people there… the reports, the budget questions, that whole thing after the award. I don’t walk around thinking strangers are watching me or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you think anyone else has noticed that your coworkers are trying to discredit you?\n\nPatient: Maybe. I mean… I think some people can see it’s not just routine. A couple coworkers get quiet when the reports come up, like they know what’s going on but don’t want to get involved. I can’t prove what they’ve noticed, but it feels coordinated.\n\nDoctor: Have these feelings about being targeted at work changed in intensity over time since the county safety award?\n\nPatient: It’s been… about a year now, I guess. [
  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n16\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF22, F60.0, F32.8\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I’m here because things at work have gotten… uncomfortable. Since that county safety award, some people have been messing with my inspection reports, or twisting them, and asking these pointed budget questions like they’re trying to make me look crooked. My supervisor says it’s just normal review, but I don’t think that’s all it is. It feels coordinated, like they’re trying to discredit me.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any proof that your coworkers are actually altering your reports or is it just a feeling you have?\n\nPatient: I mean… I don’t have a recording or anything like that, if that’s what you mean. But the reports come back with things emphasized differently, and then right after that they start asking these budget questions that make it sound like I did something wrong. Since the award, it’s been certain people, not random. My supervisor says it’s just normal review, but… I can tell it’s coordinated.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people at work are targeting you specifically for a reason?\n\nPatient: Yeah. I think it’s because of that county safety award. After that, certain people started looking at my reports differently, twisting things around, asking budget questions like they were trying to make me look dishonest. My supervisor says it’s normal review, but… it doesn’t feel normal. It feels like they’re trying to discredit me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people at work are out to get you personally, or is it just about your work performance?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s just performance. If it were, it wouldn’t have started right after the safety award, and it wouldn’t be the same people twisting my reports and asking those budget questions like I’m doing something crooked. My supervisor says it’s normal review, but… it feels more coordinated than that. Like they’re trying to discredit me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any other changes in how your coworkers interact with you outside of these report reviews and budget questions?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah. Compliance reviews have gotten more tense, and mentoring the younger inspectors isn’t as easy as it used to be. I’ve also… pulled back from a few coworkers I used to trust. I just don’t feel comfortable around them the same way.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people at work are capable of plotting against you without anyone else noticing?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think they could. It doesn’t have to be obvious. Just enough people asking the same pointed questions, changing how they look at my reports… after that award, it started to feel coordinated. My supervisor says it’s routine, but I don’t buy that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people at work are acting against you because of something you've done, or for no logical reason at all?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s for no reason. It started after that county safety award. Since then, certain people have been twisting my inspection reports and asking budget questions like I’m doing something wrong. My supervisor calls it normal review, but… it feels like they’re trying to make me look crooked.\n\nDoctor: Have you considered the possibility that your supervisor is correct and the changes at work are just part of a normal review process?\n\nPatient: I’ve considered it, yeah. I mean… that’s what he keeps saying. But it doesn’t feel normal to me. Not after the award. The way they’ve been going over my reports and asking those budget questions—it feels too pointed, like they’re trying to make me look crooked. I can’t just ignore that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people at work would be doing this to you if you hadn't received the county safety award?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. It really started after that award. Before that, they weren’t picking through my reports like this or asking those budget questions in that way. It feels like that’s what put a target on me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're being watched or targeted even when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: Not really outside of work, no. It’s mostly tied to the people there… the reports, the budget questions, that whole thing after the award. I don’t walk around thinking strangers are watching me or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you think anyone else has noticed that your coworkers are trying to discredit you?\n\nPatient: Maybe. I mean… I think some people can see it’s not just routine. A couple coworkers get quiet when the reports come up, like they know what’s going on but don’t want to get involved. I can’t prove what they’ve noticed, but it feels coordinated.\n\nDoctor: Have these feelings about being targeted at work changed in intensity over time since the county safety award?\n\nPatient: It’s been… about a year now, I guess. Since I got recognized for that county safety thing. It hasn’t really gone away.\n\nDoctor: Have you had difficulty performing your job or interacting with others because of these feelings of being targeted?\n\nPatient: Yeah… some. I still do the job, but compliance reviews feel more tense now. And mentoring the newer inspectors isn’t as easy as it used to be.\n\nI’ve also kind of pulled back from a few coworkers I used to trust. I just keep things more to myself.\n\nDoctor: How have these situations at work affected your mood or emotional state?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… it’s made me more on edge, I guess. I try not to let it get to me, but some days I’m just tired of having to watch what I say. I don’t really talk about it much.\n\nDoctor: Have these feelings of being targeted at work significantly interfered with your ability to enjoy your personal life or relationships outside of work?\n\nPatient: Not too much, I guess. I mean, I still get to work on time, do my inspections, go to church, take care of myself.\n\nIt’s mostly… if people start bringing up those reports, or asking about my spending, then it can get uncomfortable. But outside of that, I manage.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your perception of being targeted at work could be an overreaction or misinterpretation of your coworkers' actions?\n\nPatient: I’ve thought about that, but… no, I don’t think it’s just me overreacting.\n\nEver since that county safety award, certain people at work have been twisting my inspection reports, asking those pointed budget questions like they’re trying to make me look crooked. My supervisor says it’s normal review, but it doesn’t feel normal. It feels coordinated… like they’re trying to discredit me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.   },

I think it’s because of that county safety award. After that, certain people started looking at my reports differently, twisting things around, asking budget questions like they were trying to make me look dishonest. If it were, it wouldn’t have started right after the safety award, and it wouldn’t be the same people twisting my reports and asking those budget questions like I’m doing something crooked. Like they’re trying to discredit me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any other changes in how your coworkers interact with you outside of these report reviews and budget questions?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah. Compliance reviews have gotten more tense, and mentoring the younger inspectors isn’t as easy as it used to be. I’ve also… pulled back from a few coworkers I used to trust. I just don’t feel comfortable around them the same way.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people at work are capable of plotting against you without anyone else noticing?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think they could. It doesn’t have to be obvious. Just enough people asking the same pointed questions, changing how they look at my reports… after that award, it started to feel coordinated. My supervisor says it’s routine, but I don’t buy that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people at work are acting against you because of something you've done, or for no logical reason at all?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s for no reason. It started after that county safety award. Since then, certain people have been twisting my inspection reports and asking budget questions like I’m doing something wrong. My supervisor calls it normal review, but… it feels like they’re trying to make me look crooked.\n\nDoctor: Have you considered the possibility that your supervisor is correct and the changes at work are just part of a normal review process?\n\nPatient: I’ve considered it, yeah. I try not to let it get to me, but some days I’m just tired of having to watch what I say. I don’t really talk about it much.\n\nDoctor: Have these feelings of being targeted at work significantly interfered with your ability to enjoy your personal life or relationships outside of work?\n\nPatient: Not too much, I guess. My supervisor says it’s normal review, but… it doesn’t feel normal. It feels like they’re trying to discredit me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people at work are out to get you personally, or is it just about your work performance?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s just performance. If it were, it wouldn’t have started right after the safety award, and it wouldn’t be the same people twisting my reports and asking those budget questions like I’m doing something crooked. My supervisor says it’s normal review, but… it feels more coordinated than that. Like they’re trying to discredit me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any other changes in how your coworkers interact with you outside of these report reviews and budget questions?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah. Compliance reviews have gotten more tense, and mentoring the younger inspectors isn’t as easy as it used to be. I’ve also… pulled back from a few coworkers I used to trust. I just don’t feel comfortable around them the same way.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people at work are capable of plotting against you without anyone else noticing?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think they could. It doesn’t have to be obvious. Just enough people asking the same pointed questions, changing how they look at my reports… after that award, it started to feel coordinated. My supervisor says it’s routine, but I don’t buy that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people at work are acting against you because of something you've done, or for no logical reason at all?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s for no reason. It started after that county safety award. Since then, certain people have been twisting my inspection reports and asking budget questions like I’m doing something wrong. My supervisor calls it normal review, but… it feels like they’re trying to make me look crooked.\n\nDoctor: Have you considered the possibility that your supervisor is correct and the changes at work are just part of a normal review process?\n\nPatient: I’ve considered it, yeah. I mean… that’s what he keeps saying. But it doesn’t feel normal to me. Not after the award. The way they’ve been going over my reports and asking those budget questions—it feels too pointed, like they’re trying to make me look crooked. I can’t just ignore that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people at work would be doing this to you if you hadn't received the county safety award?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. It really started after that award. Before that, they weren’t picking through my reports like this or asking those budget questions in that way. It feels like that’s what put a target on me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're being watched or targeted even when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: Not really outside of work, no. It’s mostly tied to the people there… the reports, the budget questions, that whole thing after the award. I don’t walk around thinking strangers are watching me or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you think anyone else has noticed that your coworkers are trying to discredit you?\n\nPatient: Maybe. I mean… I think some people can see it’s not just routine. A couple coworkers get quiet when the reports come up, like they know what’s going on but don’t want to get involved. I can’t prove what they’ve noticed, but it feels coordinated.\n\nDoctor: Have these feelings about being targeted at work changed in intensity over time since the county safety award?\n\nPatient: It’s been… about a year now, I guess. Since I got recognized for that county safety thing. It hasn’t really gone away.\n\nDoctor: Have you had difficulty performing your job or interacting with others because of these feelings of being targeted?\n\nPatient: Yeah… some. I still do the job, but compliance reviews feel more tense now. And mentoring the newer inspectors isn’t as easy as it used to be.\n\nI’ve also kind of pulled back from a few coworkers I used to trust. I just keep things more to myself.\n\nDoctor: How have these situations at work affected your mood or emotional state?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… it’s made me more on edge, I guess. I try not to let it get to me, but some days I’m just tired of having to watch what I say. I don’t really talk about it much.\n\nDoctor: Have these feelings of being targeted at work significantly interfered with your ability to enjoy your personal life or relationships outside of work?\n\nPatient: Not too much, I guess. I mean, I still get to work on time, do my inspections, go to church, take care of myself.\n\nIt’s mostly… if people start bringing up those reports, or asking about my spending, then it can get uncomfortable. But outside of that, I manage.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your perception of being targeted at work could be an overreaction or misinterpretation of your coworkers' actions?\n\nPatient: I’ve thought about that, but… no, I don’t think it’s just me overreacting.\n\nEver since that county safety award, certain people at work have been twisting my inspection reports, asking those pointed budget questions like they’re trying to make me look crooked. My supervisor says it’s normal review, but it doesn’t feel normal. It feels coordinated… like they’re trying to discredit me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you were being targeted or conspired against before this situation at work started?\n\nPatient: No… not really before this. It’s been mostly this past year, since I got recognized for that county safety initiative. That’s when things started feeling different.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts that people were out to get you before the situation at work started?\n\nPatient: No… not before all this. It’s really just been about a year, since I got recognized for that county safety initiative. That’s when it started.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

It feels coordinated, like they’re trying to discredit me.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any proof that your coworkers are actually altering your reports or is it just a feeling you have?\n\nPatient: I mean… I don’t have a recording or anything like that, if that’s what you mean. But the reports come back with things emphasized differently, and then right after that they start asking these budget questions that make it sound like I did something wrong. Since the award, it’s been certain people, not random. My supervisor says it’s just normal review, but… I can tell it’s coordinated.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people at work are targeting you specifically for a reason?\n\nPatient: Yeah. I think it’s because of that county safety award. After that, certain people started looking at my reports differently, twisting things around, asking budget questions like they were trying to make me look dishonest. My supervisor says it’s normal review, but… it doesn’t feel normal. It feels like they’re trying to discredit me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people at work are out to get you personally, or is it just about your work performance?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s just performance. If it were, it wouldn’t have started right after the safety award, and it wouldn’t be the same people twisting my reports and asking those budget questions like I’m doing something crooked. My supervisor says it’s normal review, but… it feels more coordinated than that. Like they’re trying to discredit me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any other changes in how your coworkers interact with you outside of these report reviews and budget questions?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah. Compliance reviews have gotten more tense, and mentoring the younger inspectors isn’t as easy as it used to be. I’ve also… pulled back from a few coworkers I used to trust. I just don’t feel comfortable around them the same way.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people at work are capable of plotting against you without anyone else noticing?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think they could. It doesn’t have to be obvious. Just enough people asking the same pointed questions, changing how they look at my reports… after that award, it started to feel coordinated. My supervisor says it’s routine, but I don’t buy that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people at work are acting against you because of something you've done, or for no logical reason at all?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s for no reason. It started after that county safety award. Since then, certain people have been twisting my inspection reports and asking budget questions like I’m doing something wrong. My supervisor calls it normal review, but… it feels like they’re trying to make me look crooked.\n\nDoctor: Have you considered the possibility that your supervisor is correct and the changes at work are just part of a normal review process?\n\nPatient: I’ve considered it, yeah. I mean… that’s what he keeps saying. But it doesn’t feel normal to me. Not after the award. The way they’ve been going over my reports and asking those budget questions—it feels too pointed, like they’re trying to make me look crooked. I can’t just ignore that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people at work would be doing this to you if you hadn't received the county safety award?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. It really started after that award. Before that, they weren’t picking through my reports like this or asking those budget questions in that way. It feels like that’s what put a target on me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're being watched or targeted even when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: Not really outside of work, no. It’s mostly tied to the people there… the reports, the budget questions, that whole thing after the award. I don’t walk around thinking strangers are watching me or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you think anyone else has noticed that your coworkers are trying to discredit you?\n\nPatient: Maybe. I mean… I think some people can see it’s not just routine. A couple coworkers get quiet when the reports come up, like they know what’s going on but don’t want to get involved. I can’t prove what they’ve noticed, but it feels coordinated.\n\nDoctor: Have these feelings about being targeted at work changed in intensity over time since the county safety award?\n\nPatient: It’s been… about a year now, I guess. Since I got recognized for that county safety thing. It hasn’t really gone away.\n\nDoctor: Have you had difficulty performing your job or interacting with others because of these feelings of being targeted?\n\nPatient: Yeah… some. I still do the job, but compliance reviews feel more tense now. And mentoring the newer inspectors isn’t as easy as it used to be.\n\nI’ve also kind of pulled back from a few coworkers I used to trust. I just keep things more to myself.\n\nDoctor: How have these situations at work affected your mood or emotional state?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… it’s made me more on edge, I guess. I try not to let it get to me, but some days I’m just tired of having to watch what I say. I don’t really talk about it much.\n\nDoctor: Have these feelings of being targeted at work significantly interfered with your ability to enjoy your personal life or relationships outside of work?\n\nPatient: Not too much, I guess. I mean, I still get to work on time, do my inspections, go to church, take care of myself.\n\nIt’s mostly… if people start bringing up those reports, or asking about my spending, then it can get uncomfortable. But outside of that, I manage.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your perception of being targeted at work could be an overreaction or misinterpretation of your coworkers' actions?\n\nPatient: I’ve thought about that, but… no, I don’t think it’s just me overreacting.\n\nEver since that county safety award, certain people at work have been twisting my inspection reports, asking those pointed budget questions like they’re trying to make me look crooked. My supervisor says it’s normal review, but it doesn’t feel normal. It feels coordinated… like they’re trying to discredit me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you were being targeted or conspired against before this situation at work started?\n\nPatient: No… not really before this. It’s been mostly this past year, since I got recognized for that county safety initiative. That’s when things started feeling different.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts that people were out to get you before the situation at work started?\n\nPatient: No… not before all this. It’s really just been about a year, since I got recognized for that county safety initiative. That’s when it started.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these feelings of being targeted will ever stop or go away?\n\nPatient: I don’t know. I guess it would stop if they stopped trying to make me look crooked.\n\nBut as long as they keep twisting my reports and asking those budget questions like that… and everyone keeps acting like it’s just normal review… I don’t really see it going away.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that you're misinterpreting your coworkers' actions and they're not actually trying to discredit you?\n\nPatient: I mean… I’ve thought about that. But it doesn’t fit with what’s been happening.\n\nAfter that county safety award, certain people started twisting my inspection reports and asking these pointed budget questions, like they’re trying to make me look crooked. My supervisor says it’s just normal review, but… I can tell there’s more to it. It feels coordinated."
